At first she felt unusually energized and driven, starting side projects on predictive modeling and volunteering to lead extra cross-functional meetings, which her managers praised as initiative. When the project ended and the workload abruptly normalized, she was left feeling strangely flat and found it harder to sustain her usual focus on reports and presentations; minor setbacks at work began to feel overwhelming, and she started skipping her trail club hikes and yoga classes. After two months of this downturn, her husband and a close colleague both commented that she \"wasn't herself\" anymore and urged her to see a mental health professional when she admitted that the joy she used to find in her work and hobbies seemed to have disappeared.\n- Chief Complaint: I don’t enjoy my job or any of the things I used to love doing anymore, and it’s been like this for weeks.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days I wake up with this heavy, low feeling in my chest, like a gray filter has been laid over everything, and no matter what happens at work or at home I just feel flat and vaguely hopeless about where my life is going. \n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): Even when I block off time for things I used to love—digging into a new through the motions. \n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): Lately my body feels like it’s moving through molasses—my husband jokes that I shuffle around the house, and even at the office I catch myself just out of me, and just walking from my car to the office or sitting through a meeting leaves me feeling like I’ve run a marathon. \n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I keep telling myself that I’m screwing everything up—that I let my team down after the big relaunch by not keeping up the same pace, that I’m a burden on my husband because I’m so checked out, and that I don’t really deserve the praise or promotions I’ve gotten. \n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Simple tasks that used to be automatic, like comparing two sets of survey results or deciding what to make for dinner, now feel overwhelming, and I find myself rereading the same slide or staring at the pantry for ten minutes because my brain just won’t settle on anything. \n- S032 (Death_Suicide): There are nights when I lie in bed thinking it wouldn’t be so bad if I just didn’t wake up in the morning, and on the rougher days I catch myself idly wondering how I could make it look like an accident, even though I haven’t actually (Increased_Activity): During that project I was buzzing with energy—I’d work late, come home excited, start sketching out extra analytics ideas at midnight, and still feel like I couldn’t sit still long enough to watch a TV show with my husband. \n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): At the height of the relaunch, I honestly believed I was the only one in the company who really understood our customers and that if people would just get out of my way I could basically reinvent the whole brand on my own. \n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I was in that high gear, I couldn’t stay focused on anything—I'd be in the middle of presenting a deck and suddenly notice someone’s pen tapping or a Slack notification popping up and completely lose my train of thought, bouncing from one idea to the next without finishing what I started. \n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I signed myself up for everything during that period—leading extra cross-functional meetings, starting a side project on predictive modeling, mentoring a junior analyst, even organizing happy hours—because I felt this constant push to keep doing more and saying yes to every new idea that popped into my head.\n▸ missed a few informal deadlines on reports I used to turn in early, and at home I’ve been bailing on social plans and chores so often that my husband has started picking up most of the slack.\n- duration: Looking back, these big swings between those revved-up stretches and these long, heavy downturns have been happening off and on for several years, not just with this last product Even though I was distracted and buzzing with way too much energy, I didn't experience any real breakdown in my functioning until these low periods hit.\n\nDoctor: During these heavy, low periods you described, have you ever felt so deeply depressed that you were unable to get out of bed, take care of basic needs, or continue functioning at work for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes, this current low period has been dragging on for about six months now, which is much longer than two weeks. While I still manage to drag myself to work, my body feels like it's moving through molasses and my performance reviews have slipped because I'm missing deadlines. At home, I've been bailing on chores so much that my husband has had to pick up almost all the slack.\n\nDoctor: During these long, heavy downturns, have you ever felt that life was not worth living, or have you had any thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: To be honest, there are nights when I lie in bed thinking it wouldn't be so bad if I just didn't wake up in the morning. On the really rough days, I've even idly wondered how I could make it look like an accident. But I want to be clear that I haven't actually made any plans or done anything to act on those thoughts.\n\nDoctor: During these six months of feeling low, have you experienced other symptoms like significant changes in your appetite, weight, or excessive feelings of worthlessness or guilt?\n\nPatient: I haven't noticed any major changes in my appetite or weight, but I constantly feel this overwhelming guilt, like I'm letting my team down after the relaunch and being a burden to my husband. I keep telling myself that I'm screwing everything up and that I don't really deserve any of the praise or promotions I've received.\n\nDoctor: During these six months, have you experienced any periods where you were completely unable to function at work or perform basic self-care, or have you been able to maintain at least a minimal level of daily activity throughout?\n\nPatient: I haven't been completely bedridden, but my ability to function has definitely declined. I still go to work, but I'm moving like I'm in molasses, missing deadlines, and my manager has started asking if I'm okay. At home, I've been bailing on chores and social plans so much that my husband has had to take over almost all the daily responsibilities."

Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.
